Applicant: Seu Expiry 7 May 2009

Case Number: 07-04-2009-144242-005

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

I would be most grateful for a PDF copy of the [Rendlesham] file

Background

A Redacted version of the Rendlesham file was placed on the MoD website for public
viewing before being removed in late 2007/early 2008 for copyright reasons. The
applicant is referring to this.

The Rendlesham file was released on 4 occasions to date in 2009 and is one of the
next batch of files due to be released to The National Archives later this year.

The information on the Rendlesham file is non contentious. However, such is the
fame of this incident and public interest in the case, that a great many names of
indivduals involved are now already in the public domain and it makes little sense to
withhold them unnecessarily. It would also open the MoD up to possible censure and
ridicule. Therefore, a number of names and addresses have not been withheld. This
is consistent with previous releases of the file and that intended for publication.
Redactions have already been agreed with DG Info. .

The following Exemption(s) and/or Exception(s) are proposed as appropriate for this
case (list and provide justification):

Information not FOIA Reason for application of exemption | PIT carried
disclosed (detail) exemption out? (Y/N)'

The following comments will be made to the applicant

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 7 April 2009 asking for a copy
of the Rendlesham file.

In accordance with your e-mail of the same date, please find enclosed a disk
containing a copy of the Rendlesham File. Some personal information such as
names, addresses and telephone numbers has been withheld under exemption s.40
(Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000. As this exemption is

1 If Yes, please attach details of PIT.

e@ an absolute exemption an assessment of the public interest for and against
disclosure is not required under the terms of the Act.

You may wish to note that the file is a compilation file comprised of documents that
were removed from their original file and placed on this file. This would account for
the non sequential enclosure numbering. Although the file cover states the file was
opened on 25 October 1982, it is clear that this was not the case. Sec(AS) was not in
being at this time, only being formed in early 1985 and therefore no file with a
reference number commencing with D/Sec(AS) could have been in existence in 1982.
No record of when the file was created can be found.

The original the enclosed file is one of 160 due for transfer to The National Archives
as part of an ongoing three year programme that was featured quite heavily in the
UK media over 2008, to make our UFO files from 1979 to 2007 available to the
general public,

The MOD has taken steps to keep this disk and any attachments free from viruses.
However it accepts no liability for any loss or damage howsoever caused as a result
of any virus being passed on. It is the responsibility of the recipient to perform all
necessary checks.

I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 7 April 2009 asking for a copy of the
Rendlesham file.

In accordance with your e-mail of the same date, please find enclosed a disk containing a
copy of the Rendlesham file. Some personal information such as names, addresses and
telephone numbers has been withheld under exemption s.40 (Personal Information) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000. As this exemption is an absolute exemption an assessment
of the public interest for and against disclosure is not required under the terms of the Act.

You may wish to note that the file is a compilation file comprised of documents that were
removed from their original file and placed on this file. This would account for the non
sequential enclosure numbering. Although the file cover states the file was opened on 25
October 1982, it is clear that this was not the case. Sec(AS) was not in being at this time, only
being formed in early 1985 and therefore no file with a reference number commencing with
D/Sec(AS) could have been in existence in 1982. No record of when the file was created can
be found. : -

_ The original of the enclosed file is one of 160 due for transfer to The National Archives as part
of an ongoing three year programme that was featured quite heavily in the UK media over
2008, to make our UFO files from 1979 to 2007 available to the general public,

The MOD has taken steps to keep this disk and any attachments free from viruses. However
it accepts no liability for any loss or damage howsoever caused as a result of any virus being
passed on. It is the responsibility of the recipient to perform all necessary checks.

On Apr 6, 2009, at 6:54 a Ba wrote:
Dea

Thank you for your e-mail of 24 March 2009 asking how you can obtain the Ministry of
Defence (MoD) documents on the Rendlesham Forest Incident of 1980. Your e-mail is
being treated as a formal request for information under the Freedom of Information Act
2000.

Providing you with all documentation the MoD holds on the Rendlesham incident
would involve manually checking, as a minimum, some 100 UFO files, and then
preparing relevant documents for release, the cost of which would exceed far the £600
limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by
Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of Defence is not obliged to comply with your
request.

However, the MoD has a file on this incident, that was created sometime after the
incident and which is currently being prepared for release to The National Archives as
part of a programme to transfer some 160 MOD files on UFO matters. The MoD had.
previously placed the file on its own website, but has had to remove this for copyright
reasons. If you would like a copy of our file on Rendlesham, please let me know
whether you prefer a PDF copy on disk (our preferred option as it saves on paper and
postage costs) or a hard copy, together with your address and I will forward it to you. I
am afraid that due to the size of the file, I am unable to e-mail it to you.

You may wish to be aware that the MOD has already released a great deal of

07/04/2009

on Page 2 of 3

@ information about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were
routinely destroyed after five years until 1967 when they were generally preserved for
The National Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these together with
records up to 1992 are now available for public viewing. The National Archives can be
contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, SurreyTW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876
3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the records
they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet
at: http://Awww.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

cKgroun

The MoD has a single file on the Cosford incident which is due for transfer to The
National Archives later this year as part of the UFO File release programme.

A PIT examining the case for withholding the file on the grounds it is due for future
publication is outlined below and will be included in the response.

The following Exemption(s) and/or Exception(s) are proposed as appropriate for this
case (list and provide justification):

Information not FOIA Reason for application of exemption | PIT carried

disclosed (detail) exemption out?
Specify cviny™
section

MoD File on Cosford 22 Information intended for future Y

incident publication

The following comments will be made to the applicant:

Thank you for your Freedom of Information (FOT) request dated 12 Apri! 2009 asking

for all files relating to the Cosford UFO Incident.

The Ministry of Defence (MoD) has a single file (D/Sec(AS)/12/7 Part A) covering this

topic which is due for transfer to The National Archives later this year, where it will
be available for public viewing.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future
publication) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to
comply with this part of your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption,
meaning that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the
information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this file being released to the public as
it will enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this incident,

(11 1 Yes, please attach details of PIT.
there is no overriding reason why the public should have the information
immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing files in chronological order,
the public will be able to follow the role of the MoD in a more logical and orderly
fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information
is planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this file
will undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other
members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have
to be put off.

Finally, the release programme itself is complicated and the release of files out of
sequence will result in unnecessary confusion for the MoD, The National Archives
and, indeed, the pubiic when they are attempting to track files once they are
transferred.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to
release these files at this moment

I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

Dear ZG)

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 9 April 2009 asking for details of UFO
sightings for 2008. —

Firstly, it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports

of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have |
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s |
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. |

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source,
and to date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as
aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains
totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

Details of UFO sightings for 2008 are on the MoD website, however, as you do not have a
computer, | have printed off the details for you and have included details of sightings logged
on our database for 2009 to date.

| hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent
internal review by contacting the Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main

Dear IRN

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 6 April 2009 asking for
information regarding UFOs in Birmingham and the rest of the country over the last year.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of “UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

Details of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 can be found by searching
under UFO Reports on the MoD website at www.mod.uk. I attach a spreadsheet showing details of
UFO sightings recorded by the MoD to date for 2009. You will note a few reports for 2008 that were
not received in time to be placed on the MoD website. Additionally, in a number of cases, no firm
date was given for the sighting and only the month is given.

I hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
ClO-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www.ico.gov.uk.

evaluative study of the Freedom of Information Act 2000 in co-operation with a number of public

explained in the survey. The Ministry of Defence will only see the published results: no individual

From: [SIRO

Deo

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 26 March 2009 asking how
many UFO sightings had there been in Scotland and Edinburgh, how much the government spends
on replying or dealing with UFO sightings and what is the procedure for a reported sighting?

You have not given a timeframe for the number of sightings in Edinburgh and Scotland, but details
of UFO sightings reported to the MoD for the period 1997-2008 can be found on the Ministry of
Defence (MoD) website www.mod.uk by searching under the phrase “UFO Reports”. To date this
year, there have been two sightings near Edinburgh and nine in the rest of Scotland.

The MoD, which has the lead across government in dealing with UFO matters, employs one full time
civil servant (myself) dedicated to dealing with the subject of UFOs. The post is at D Grade, which is
the most junior management level within the Ministry. Whilst it would be inappropriate to provide
individual salary details, the pay range for D grade posts is £20,815-£27,159 per year.

In addition to my post, I receive some administrative support from an El Grade civil servant and my
civil service line manager at C1 grade has oversight of my work. Approximately 20% of the E1’s
and C1’s time is spent on UFO related matters. The annual pay range for an El is £15,847-£20,074
and for the Cl it is £32,623-£41,325. All three posts attract a standard local recruitment and retention
allowance for RAF High Wycombe of £2000 per year. As a rough guide therefore, based on the
highest annual salary, total costs are in the order of £40,000 per year.

In the majority of cases, the procedure for dealing with UFO reports is to record the details of the
sighting and to write to the individual concerned, explaining the limited involvement of the MoD in
UFO matters. At the beginning of each year we place details of the preceding year’s sightings on the
MoD website.

On occasion we do investigate reports. This usually involves liaising with air defence experts (such
as radar specialists) on, perhaps, half a dozen reports per year. These will generally be sightings
made by aircrew, members of the armed forces or the police. However, to date, the MoD knows of
no evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

T hope this is helpful.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
CIO-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www. ico.gov.uk.

From: [Enema a

Sent: 08 April 2009 09:10

Subject: Release-Authorised: 20090408-FO! Response Siamamma UH Os 12-03-2009-145828-003

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 12 March 2009 asking for any
details of UFOs.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence. we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should atid that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

Turning to your request, the MoD has over 170 UFO files and the costs of preparing them for release
would far exceed the £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act and, as
provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of Defence is not obliged to comply with your
request.

However, you may wish to be aware that the MOD has already released a great deal of information
about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after five
years until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few have
survived before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public
viewing. They can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone,
020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the records they
hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at www-nationalarchives.gov.uk.

The MoD currently has a programme to transfer some 160 UFO files for the period up to 2007 to
The National Archives by the close of 2010. It is hoped that files for the period 1992-2000 will be
transferred by the end of this year and they will be made available for public viewing on the internet.
Details of all sightings for the period 1997-2008 are available on the MoD website www.mod.uk by
searching under “UFO Reports”. UFO sightings for 2009 will also be placed on the website in
January 2010.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
CIO-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of “UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

under UFO Reports on the MoD website at www mod .uk. | attach a spreadsheet showing details of

not received in time to be placed on the MoD website. Additionally, in a number of cases, no firm

Dear Sg

Thank you for your e-mail of 24 March 2009 asking how you can obtain the Ministry of Defence
(MoD) documents on the Rendlesham Forest Incident of 1980. Your e-mail is being treated as a
formal request for information under the Freedom of Information Act 2000.

Providing you with all documentation the MoD holds on the Rendlesham incident would involve
manually checking, as a minimum, some 100 UFO files, and then preparing relevant documents for
release, the cost of which would exceed far the £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of
Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of Defence is not obliged to
comply with your request.

However, the MoD has a file on this incident, that was created sometime after the incident and which
is currently being prepared for release to The National Archives as part of a programme to transfer
some 160 MOD files on UFO matters. The MoD had previously placed the file on its own website,
but has had to remove this for copyright reasons. If you would like a copy of our file on Rendlesham,
please let me know whether you prefer a PDF copy on disk (our preferred option as it saves on paper
and postage costs) or a hard copy, together with your address and I will forward it to you. I am afraid
that due to the size of the file, ] am unable to e-mail it to you.

You may wish to be aware that the MOD has already released a great deal of information about
UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after five years
until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few have survived
before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public viewing. The
National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or
telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the
records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at:
http://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

If you are not satisfied with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling
of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Head of Corporate Information, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail
ClO-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
working days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain dissatisfied following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, http://www.ico.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely,

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 24 March 2009 asking how
many UFO reports the Ministry of Defence received for 2007-2009 and for any other information on
UFOs.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was scen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

Despite a great many rumours, the MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
“UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life
forms, about which it remains totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MoD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

Details of sightings received by the MoD for the period 1997-2008 are available on the MoD website
www.mod.uk by searching under “UFO Reports”. | attach details of UFO sightings for 2009 to date.
You will note that in a number of instances we do not have an exact date for the sighting. Also we
received a handful of sightings for 2008 after we had placed the figures for that year on our website.

You may wish to be aware that the MOD has already released a great deal of information about
UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after five years
until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few have survived
before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public viewing. The
National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or
telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the
records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at
www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

The National Archives makes a small charge for people to view these files, but they placed seven
new files on their website this week and for a short period of time they are available for viewing at
no charge. The Ministry of Defence Freedom of Information website also contains some released
information on UFOs. This can be accessed the internet at www.mod.uk by searching under
“UFO”.

I hope this is helpful and good luck with your project.

e AUTHORISATION FOR APPLYING FOI EXEMPTIONS/ EIR EXCEPTIONS
Applicant aa Expiry 31 March 2009
Case Number: 03-03-2009-105321-005 S

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

A copy of the official case file for the Cosford Incident. I understand that the
documents were withdrawn from the MoD website for copyright reasons.

Background

Alongside the Rendlesham Incident, the Cosford Incident is probably the most
famous UFO incident in the UK. A number of redacted documents relating to the
Cosford incident were placed on the MoD website for public viewing before being
removed in late 2007/early 2008 for copyright reasons. These documents comprised
the bulk of the documents on the MoD file but the file itself was not released. Whilst
confusing the two, the applicant has clearly asked for the file. There is no reason
why the applicant cannot be provided with the documents that previously appeared
on the website. However, the residual documents, which together with those
published already form the Cosford file, should be withheld under exemption s.22 as
it is one of the batch of files due for transfer to The National Archives later this year.

The Cosford file itself was withheld on 2 occasions in 2008 under exemption s.22 but
the documentation previously on the website was released 7 times in 2008.

The information on the Cosford file is largely non contentious but two documents,
already in the public domain following earlier FOI requests, can be misinterpreted as
an admission that the MoD believed an alien craft penetrated UK airspace. In fact,
the MoD concern was centred on the alleged Aurora aircraft. All redactions on the file
and those on the documents previously placed on the website are made under
exemption s.40 and have already been agreed with CMem.

The following Exemption(s) and/or Exception(s) are proposed as appropriate for this
case (list and provide justification):

Information not FOIA Reason for application of exemption | PIT carried
disclosed (detail) exemption out? (Y/N)'
Specify
section

including names, 40 Personal data No.
telephone numbers, room Absolute
numbers etc Exemption
Cosford File 22 Information intended for future Y
publication

The following comments will be made to the applicant

1 If Yes, please attach details of PIT.

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 3 March 2009 asking for a
copy of the official case file of the Cosford Incident in 1993.

A number of Cosford related documents were placed on the Ministry of Defence
website but later withdrawn for copyright reasons, as you say. The documents are
too large for me to e-mail to you but I can send you a copy on disk or paper. In
order to save on paper and postage costs, our preferred option would be to provide
you with the information on disk, however, the choice is yours. Please let me know
which you would prefer.

I should make it clear that whilst these documents constitute the bulk of the MoD file
on the subject, the full file is currently being prepared for transfer to The National
Archives as part of a major programme to transfer some 160 UFO files covering the
late 1970s to 2007. It is expected the file will be available to the public later this
year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future
publication) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to
comply with this part of your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption,
meaning that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the
information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this file being released to the public as
it will enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this incident,
there is no overriding reason why the public should have the information
immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing files in chronological order,
the public will be able to follow the role of the MoD in a more logical and orderly
fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information
is planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this file
will undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other
members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have
to be put off.

Finally, the release programme itself is complicated and the release of files out of
sequence will result in unnecessary confusion for the MoD, The National Archives
and, indeed, the public when they are attempting to track files once they are
transferred.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to
release these files at this moment. However, J do hope that the information we can
provide on the Cosford Incident will be helpful to you.
I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that the

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this file being released to the public as it will enable
them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this incident, there is no overriding reason
why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing

Dear IS

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 23 February 2009 asking for copies of any
and all documentation regarding the alleged UFO incident at Rendlesham Forrest in 1980. The other
aspects of your request will be dealt with by another branch.

Answering your request would involve manually checking, as a minimum, some 100 UFO files, and
then preparing relevant documents for release, the cost of which would exceed far the £600 limit set
for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the
Ministry of Defence is not obliged to comply with your request.

Whilst it is not possible to quantify without undertaking a major administrative exercise, my
experience would suggest that the overwhelming majority of documents mentioning the Rendlesham
incident are requests for information or correspondence from members of the public such as
yourself, which may not provide you with the information you are looking for. However, the MoD
has a file on this incident, that was created sometime after the incident and which is currently being
prepared for release to The National Archives as part of a programme to transfer some 160 MOD
files on UFO matters. The MoD had previously placed the file on its own website, but has had to
remove this for copyright reasons. If you would like a copy of our file on Rendlesham, please let me
know whether you prefer a PDF copy on disk (our preferred option as it saves on paper and postage
costs) or a hard copy, together with your address and I will forward it to you. I am afraid that due to
the size of the file, I am unable to e-mail it to you.

You may wish to be aware that the MOD has already released a great deal of information about
UFOs which is available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after five years
until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few have survived
before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for public viewing. The
National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or
telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the
records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at:
http/Avww.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the

Director of Information Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-

mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

http://www. informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

I would be grateful for a copy of the Rendlesham documents removed from the MoD
website

Background

A Redacted version of the Rendlesham file was placed on the MoD website for public
viewing before being removed in late 2007/early 2008 for copyright reasons. The
applicant is referring to this.

The Rendlesham file was released on 2 occasions to date in 2009 and is one of the
next batch of files due to be released to The National Archives later this year.

The information on the Rendlesham file is non contentious. However, such is the
fame of this incident and public interest in the case, that a great many names of
indivduals involved are now already in the public domain and it makes little sense to
withhold them unnecessarily. It would also open the MoD up to possible censure and
ridicule. Therefore, a number of names and addresses have not been withheld. This
is consistent with previous releases of the file and that intended for publication.
Redactions have already been agreed with DG Info.

case (list and provide justification):

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 28 February 2009 asking for a
copy of documents on the Rendlesham Incident removed from the Ministry of
Defence (MoD) website.

In accordance with your e-mail of 3 March 2009, please find enclosed a disk
containing a copy of the Rendiesham File. Some personal information such as

1 If Yes, please attach details of PIT.

names, addresses and telephone numbers has been withheld under exemption s.40
(Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000. As this exemption is
an absolute exemption an assessment of the public interest for and against
disclosure is not required under the terms of the Act.

You may wish to note that the file is a compilation file comprised of documents that
were removed from their original file and placed on this file. This would account for
the non sequential enclosure numbering. Although the file cover states the file was
opened on 25 October 1982, it is clear that this was not the case. Sec(AS) was not in
being at this time, only being formed in early 1985 and therefore no file with a
reference number commencing with D/Sec(AS) could have been in existence in 1982.
No record of when the file was created can be found.

The original the enclosed file is one of 160 due for transfer to The National Archives
as part of an ongoing three year programme that was featured quite heavily in the
UK media over 2008, to make our UFO files from 1979 to 2007 available to the
general public,

The MOD has taken steps to keep this disk and any attachments free from viruses.

of any virus being passed on. It is the responsibility of the recipient to perform all
necessary checks.

Sent: 10 March 2009 10:20
Subject: Release-Authorised:20090310-FOI Response SaMiaimmetir O Reports 12-02-2009-
121150-002

Attachments: 20090310-Attachmen {gM 12-02-2009121150-002.xIs

Dea

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 11 February 2009 asking for details of UFO
sightings reported to the Ministry of Defence.

Details of UFO sightings for the period 1997-2008 are now available for public viewing on the MoD
website www.mod.uk by searching under UFO reports. Details of sightings for 2009 will be added
in January 2010. I attach details of UFO sightings for 2009 to date. In a number of cases we do not
have the actual date of the sighting. You will also note a small number of reports covering 2008.
These were received after the bulk of the 2008 reports were placed on the website.

You may also wish to be aware that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) has already released a great deal
of information about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MoD files were routinely
destroyed after five years until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National Archives.
A few have survived before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992 are now available for
public viewing. A major programme to transfer UFO files from the Directorate of Air Staff and
Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period up to 2007, is currently underway and it is hoped this
will be completed by the end of 2010. The National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue,
Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a
website giving information about the records they hold and how to access them. This can be found
on the internet at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

I hope this is helpful.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Director of Information Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-
mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Subject: 20090211 Eieimmatinvestigation of UFOs-U
Date: Wed, 11 Feb 2009 11:15:27 +0000

From:

To:

Deo SE

Thank you for your e-mail of 25 January 2009 asking how the MoD investigates UFO reports and
whether the MoD has to investigate over extended periods of time.

The MoD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether
what was seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the
United Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorized foreign military
activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source,

and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature
of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural

12/02/2009

. Page 2 of 2

Devomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function
of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of
defence resources if we were to do so.

That having been said, we do occasionally investigate sightings reported to the MoD. This usually
involves liaising with air defence specialists (e.g. radar experts) on perhaps half a dozen sightings per
year. These will generally be sightings made by aircrew, members of the armed forces or the police.
Each case is viewed on an individual basis and it is not possible to predict how long an investigation
would take or what course it would follow. However, I can say that investigations are limited in their
nature and likely to be concluded within weeks, rather than months. I am not aware of any investigation
in recent years lasting for more than a few weeks.

L hope this is helpful.

Dear SR

Thank you for your letter of 3 February 2009 asking what Nick Pope’s
actual role was within the UFO Desk scenario.

Your letter is being treated as a request for recorded information under the Freedom of
Information Act 2000. Section 1(1)(a) of the Act , subject to certain exclusions, gives any
person making a request for information to a public authority the entitlement to be
informed in writing by the public authority whether it holds information of the description
specified in the request and that section 1(1)(b), subject to certain exemptions, creates an
entitlement to receive the information held by the public authority.

Unfortunately we do not hold any recorded information relevant to your request and the
Act does not require us to create new information in order to satisfy a request. However,
in an effort to provide help and assistance (as we are obliged to do under section 16 of
the Act) | can inform you that the UFO related tasks carried out by myself are highly likely
to have been broadly similar to those originally undertaken by Mr Pope when he was in
MOD. These include the answering of parliamentary correspondence, which would
include letters from MPs and Parliamentary Questions, together with correspondence
from members of the public such as yourself, on the subject of UFOs.

Additionally, | also record UFO sightings reported to the Ministry of Defence and,
occasionally, investigate those reports to see if they have any defence significance.
However, you should bear in mind that my role may not exactly match that of Mr Pope.

| hope this is helpful. -

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an

independent internal review by contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6"

Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail Info-XD@mod.uk). Please note
that any request for an internal review must be made within 40 working days of the date
on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of
Information Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate the
case until the internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and
powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http:/Awww.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.”

Yours sincerely,

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 27 February 2009
asking for any information available on the subject of UFOs.

The Ministry of Defence has over 170 UFO related files that would need to be prepared
for release to you, the cost which would far exceed the £600 limit set for compliance with
the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of
Defence is not obliged to comply with your request.

However, although you do not state exactly what information you require, you may wish to
be aware that the Ministry of Defence (MoD) has already released a great deal of
information about UFOs which is available for public viewing. MoD files were routinely
destroyed after five years until 1967 when they were generally preserved for The National
Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these together with records up to 1992
are now available for public viewing. A major programme to transfer UFO files from the
Directorate of Air Staff and Defence Intelligence Staff covering the period up to 2007, is
currently underway and it is hoped this will be completed by the end of 2010. The
National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Richmond, Kew, Surrey TW9 4DU
or telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving
information about the records they hold-and how to access them. This can be found on
the internet at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

The MoD website also contains some released information on UFOs including details of
UFO sightings for the period 1997-2008. This can be accessed the internet at
www.mod.uk, by searching under “UFO reports”. Details of sightings for 2009 will be
placed on the website in January 2010.

| hope this is helpful.

Dear

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 1 February 2009 asking whether there was
any activity taking place over the Rhiw on the Lleyn Peninsula 31 January 2009 as several witnesses
saw an unexplained light.

The Ministry of Defence does not keep a central record of all military aircraft activity and in order to
accurately state whether there was any military flying activity in the area, we would need to contact
some thirty flying stations across the UK, the cost which would exceed the £600 limit set for
compliance with the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the
Ministry of Defence is not obliged to comply with your request.

Whilst we do have a centralised record of aircraft entering the UK Low Flying System, this system
was closed on the day in question and I would therefore very much doubt that the sightings were
caused by military aircraft.

No other similar sightings were made for the areas in question over this period.

I hope this is helpful.

and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the

To:

Subject: 20090220 Orange Orbs-U

eo

Thank you for your e-mail of 12 February 2009 asking about low flying orange orbs
you saw near Ministry of Defence (MoD) land.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the MoD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to

date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural

23/02/2009

. Page 3 of 3

phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena. .

That having been said, if you can provide me with the dates when you saw these orbs I will try to
find out if any military activity was taking place.

Room 2E03

Spitfire Block

HQ Air Command
RAF High Wycombe

Subject: Release-Authorised:20090220 Samim Orange Orbs-U

<a

Thank you for your e-mail of 12 February 2009 asking about low flying orange orbs
you saw near Ministry of Defence (MoD) land.

Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the MoD examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no UFO report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the
function of the MoD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate
use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MoD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters or the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MoD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

That having been said, if you can provide me with the dates when you saw these orbs I will try to
find out if any military activity was taking place.

Applicant: SEU Expiry date: 27 February 2009

Case Number: 04-02-2009-091026-001

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

Any and all information from any sources (including civilian) on an unidentified radar
target reported by a Section 401] for the period just before Christmas 1994. Actual
request is attached.

Background

Any information relating to any UFO incident or sighting made in 1994-95 will be
released in due course as part of the UFO File release programme.

A PIT examining the case for withholding information relating to this incident on the
grounds it is due for future publication is outlined below and will be included in the
response.

The following Exemption(s) and/or Exception(s) are proposed as appropriate for this
case (list and provide justification):

Information not FOIA Reason for application of exemption | PIT carried
disclosed (detail) exemption out?
Specify cyiny™
section
Information relating to | 22 Information intended for future Y
UFO report byfSlailon 40 publication
ISeciion Mal

The following comments will be made to the applicant:

Any report from SE to the MoD UFO Desk should be contained in files due
for transfer to The National Archives later this year.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future
publication) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to
comply with this part of your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption,
meaning that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the
information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in these files being released to the public
as it will enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this
incident, there is no overriding reason why the public should have the information
immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing files in chronological order,

"1 If Yes, please attach details of PIT.

@.:- public will be able to follow the role of the MoD in a more logical and orderly
fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information
is planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this file
will undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other
members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have
to be put off.

Finally, the release programme itself is complicated and the release of files out of
sequence will result in unnecessary confusion for the MoD, The National Archives
and, indeed, the public when they are attempting to track files once they are
transferred.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to
release these files at this moment

I hereby give authorisation for the use of the aforementioned exemptions and/or
exceptions in this case to withhold information from the Applicant:

Dear aN

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request asking for any information held
regarding an unidentified target reported to the Ministry of Defence (MoD) by a ERIE ust
before Christmas 1994.

Any report rom 0 the MoD UFO Desk should be contained in files due for transfer
to The National Archives later this year, which will cover the period in question.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future publication) of the
Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to comply with this part of your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning that the
MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in these files being released to the public as it will enable
them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this incident, there is no overriding reason
why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could be argued, that by releasing
files in chronological order, the public will be able to follow the role of the MoD in a more logical
and orderly fashion, and to view actions in their correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is planned and
managed within the department responsible. The release of this file will undermine this release
process, and delay the release of other files (which other members of the public may be waiting for)
as work on them would necessarily have to be put off.

Finally, the release programme itself is complicated and the release of files out of sequence will
result in unnecessary confusion for the MoD, The National Archives and, indeed, the public when
they are attempting to track files once they are transferred.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release these files at
this moment

I hope this is helpful.

FREEDOM OF INFORMATION REQUEST.

Date: 23rd January, 2009.
Dear Sir/Madam,

I would respectfully like to request any and all information you have on the unidentified
radar target reported to the MoD by SIEM just before
Christmas of 1994/1195 at RAF Lyneham in Wiltshire either stored in paper or electronic
format. Please find attached a brief statement from BEIMMEIN describing the events in
question.

According fasection 40, | he first reported this incident to the MoD by telephone and later
in writing. In a recent telephone interview with him ISM also informed me that
two security personnel on duty at RAF Lyneham that night telephone the control centre and
spoke to him in person. Both security guards reported seeing this phenomenon themselves.
I’d therefore respectfully like to request any other information from any other sources ei-
ther civilian or military that you might have regarding the incident in question.

Any information concerning this event would be greatly appreciated.

Yours sincerely, |

Firstly I must stress that I am one of the worlds biggest sceptics when it comes to UFO's,
however I can offer no realistic explanation for what I saw, therefore it was literally an
unidentified flying object.

1 was working in Air Traffic control at RAF Lyneham in Wiltshire on a night shift. I was
the approach controller and supervisor of the shift. At that time I must have had almost 20
years experience of working on radar and was very used to analysing the quirks which a
radar can produce such as bird activity, anaprop (atmospheric variation) and MTI
supression.

I was recovering a couple of C130's to base when I saw a large ‘blob' on the radar screen
about 3 miles from touchdown on the centreline. I had no idea what it was so I called it to
the pilots and fed them around it. The ‘blob’ was stationary. I handed the aircraft over to
the visual controller and then went upstairs to the visual control room, where I could see
the ‘blob’ on the DFTI (Distance from touchdown indicator). I looked through the window
but couldn't see anything so I returned to the radar room. Suddenly the ‘blob’ burst and
fragmented shooting off in all directions at massive speed. Some discussion took place
between me and the visual controller and we left it at that. Some time later I received a
phone call from avery agitated airman who said ' Sir I have not been drinking, I am on
guard duty and have just seen something that was very strange and I can't explain but
thought that Air Traffic should be informed of’, He said he had seen a bright light to the
East I remember commenting that it was nearly Christmas but doubted that we would
find 3 wise men or a vitgin at Lyneham. He said that the bright light had burst and that
fragments had shot in ali directions at speeds which he could not believe. He said he had
never seen anything travel so fast and it was definitely not an aircraft. His account caused
the hairs on my neck to stand up. Several minutes later another airman telephoned and
gave a similar account. I logged all the details and telephoned the MOD UFO desk, gave
them a verbal report backed up with a written report which hopefully they will still have
on record.

This happened just before Christmas but I struggle to remember the year. I served at
Lyneham in the early to mid 90's and it would be December 1994 or 1995. I think
the visual controller was a chap called who was a Sergeant at the time but
later became commissioned. I've not really discussed the matter very much because I
cannot justify or explain it any better than I have to you.

Please call me on my mobile if you would like to discuss it more. Section 40,

Formerly Flight Lieutenant, RAF

154424-015 and 23-02-2009-153908-014-U

don

Thank you for your two Freedom of Information requests of 22 January 2009 asking about a
UFO sighting on the Isle of Lewis on January and near Bridgend on 3 January 2009. You wished to
know if there was any military activity in these areas at the time of the sightings and whether we had
received any similar reports. Please take this response to cover both requests.

The Ministry of Defence does not keep a central record of all military aircraft activity and in order to
accurately state whether there was any military activity in the area, we would need to contact some
thirty flying stations across the UK, the cost which would exceed the £600 limit set for compliance
with the Freedom of Information Act and, as provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of
Defence is not obliged to comply with your request.

Whilst we do have a centralised record of aircraft entering the UK Low Flying System, this system
was closed over the period in question and I would therefore very much doubt that the sightings were
caused by military activity.

No other similar sightings were made for the areas in question over this period.

I am sorry I can not be more helpful.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the

Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-
mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Shawbury and Cornwall in 1993, Manchester in 1995 and a number of incidents in the’
USA. Since January 2009 this branch has lead responsibility for UFO matters within the
Ministry of Defence (MoD) following a reorganisation of work and your letter has been
passed to us to answer alongside your FOI request. :

Details of UFO sightings for the period 2006-2008, which would include any Flying
Triangles are now available for viewing on the MoD website www.mod.uk by searching
under the phrase UFO Reports.

The information regarding the RAF Cosford, RAF Shawbury and Cornwall incidents is
contained in a MoD file covering the sightings in late March 1993. This file was previously
available on the MoD website but was removed for copyright reasons (it is possible you
may already have seen it on the internet). If you will let me know whether you require a
paper copy or an electronic version (our preferred option), | will arrange for it to be sent to
you.

UFO files covering the period 1967-1992 have been passed to The National Archives and
are available for public viewing on their website www.nationalarchives.gov.uk. Information
relating to the Belgian incident may be found in the files available there. However, | am
also aware that some later correspondence relating to this matter is also contained in files
due for transfer to The National Archives later this year. These files will cover the period

1992 to 2000 and will therefore also include any information we may have on the Air
France incident in 1994 and the British Airways incident near Manchester 1995.

Therefore, as provided by exemption s.22 (Information intended for future publication) of
the Freedom of Information Act 2000, the MoD is not obliged to comply with this part of
your request.

This is not the end of the matter, of course; section 22 is a qualified exemption, meaning
that the MOD must assess the balance of public interest in release of the information.

Whilst there is undoubtedly public interest in this file being released to the public as it will
enable them to have a more informed view of the MoD’s role in this incident, there is no
overriding reason why the public should have the information immediately. Indeed it could
be argued, that by releasing files in chronological order, the public will be able to follow
the role of the MoD in a more logical and orderly fashion, and to view actions in their
correct historical perspective.

It is part of effective administration of government that the publication of information is
planned and managed within the department responsible. The release of this file will
undermine this release process, and delay the release of other files (which other
members of the public may be waiting for) as work on them would necessarily have to be
put off.

Finally, the release programme itself is complicated and the release of files out of
sequence will result in unnecessary confusion for the MoD, The National Archives and,
indeed, the public when they are attempting to track files once they are transferred.

It is for these reasons that we do not believe it would be in the public interest to release
these files at this moment

Turning to the incident over the Channel Islands, the limited documentation we hold on
the matter can be viewed on the MoD website www.mod.uk by searching in our
Disclosure Log under the phrase Channel Islands.

| am not aware of the MoD holding any information relating to the incidents in the USA
that you mention. However, since a number of them are now quite old, you might wish to
look at the files in The National Archives. As explained above, our files for the year 1997
are due for transfer to The National Archives later this year. If we hold any documentation
regarding the incident in March 1997 it will be held on these files, although | very much
doubt we would hold such information. Again, exemption s.22 applies. We hold no~
information on an incident in Texas in 2008.

| hope this is helpful.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you. are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an
independent internal review by contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, oe
Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note

that any request for an internal review must be made within 40 days of the date on which
the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

!f you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint
to the Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of
Information Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your

_ case until the internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and
powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http:/Avww.informationcommissioner,gov.uk.

Yours sincere!

process is gathering the experiences and opinions of FOI requesters like you. Any information you

privacy policy explained in the survey. The Ministry of Defence will only see the published results:
no individual responses will be visible to us and your participation will have no effect on your

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

SEGIGR has asked for information regarding a wide range of UFO incidents. In
Cornwall, RAF Cosford and RAF Shawbury in 1993, Manchester in 1995, Belgium in
1989, Flying Triangle sightings for the period 2006-08, over the Channel Islands in
2007, involving an Air France airliner in 1994 together with various sightings in the
USA. Actual requests are attached.

Background

Flying Triangles are a type of UFO and sightings for the period 2006-08 are available
for viewing on the MoD website alongside any other UFO sightings. Documents
relating to the Channel Islands sighting are available for viewing on the MoD website.

Information relating to RAF Cosford, RAF Shawbury and Cornwall in 1993 will be
made available to the applicant in redacted format. The redactions, which all come
under Section 40 (Personal Information) have already been agreed with DG Info and
placed in the public domain in the same manner as the Rendlesham file.

Any information relating to the Belgium, Manchester and Air France sightings will be
released in due course as part of the UFO File release programme. As will any
information held on the US sightings, although it is doubtful there actually is any.

We have no information regarding a sighting in Texas in 2008.

A PIT examining the case for withholding information relating to the Belgium,
Manchester and Air France sightings on the grounds it is due for future publication is
outlined below and will be included in the response.

the information you requested.

that I had some difficulty in retrieving the relevant files from archive.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
this request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the
Director of Information Exploitation, 6 Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-
mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made within 40
days of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may wish to take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

REDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCUMENT

From: FY
Sent: 22 July 2008 16:15

To:
, Subject: Release-Authorised: UFO Sightings.

Dear Sir,

| am writing with reference to your report of three ‘unidentified flying objects’ seen on the 11, 12 and 14 July
2008. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to ‘UFO's.

First, it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance; namely,
whether theré is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been compromised by hostile
or unauthorised air activity. . ~ .

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no
‘UFO’ report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting
reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircratt lights or natural phenomena, could be
found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this
kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

‘ The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the question of the
existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally open-minded. | should add
that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

With regard to your three observations, | can confirm that for the 12 July 2008, we received four other UFO
sighting reports, and they were from Surrey, Gloucestershire, Lincolnshire and North Yorkshire. We are
satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached
by unauthorised aircraft.

Sorry | could not be any help.

Yours sincerely

Ministry of Defence :
Directorate of Air Staff — Freedom of Information 1
5t Floor, Zone H

Main Building

Whitehall

London

“~  SW1A2HB

E-mail — das-ufo-office @mod.uk

From: (GD

Sent: - 30 June 2008 15:44

Subject: Release-Authorised: UFO Sighting.

Dear

| am writing with reference to your report of an:‘unidentified flying object’ seen on 20 June 2008, the details of
which you passed to the Westmorland Gazette. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence relating to ‘UFO’s.

First, it may be helpful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance; namely,
whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace might have been compromised by hostile
or unauthorised air activity. Uniess there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an
external source, and to date no ‘UFO’ report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations could be found for them if
resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial:
identification service. It would be an’ inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do SO. -

. The MOD does not have any expertisé or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the question of the
existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally open-minded. | should add
that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

” With regard to your particular observation, | can confirm that we received one other report of a UFO sighting
for the 20 June 2008, and that was from Doncaster in South Yorkshire. We are satisfied that there is no
corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom’s airspace was breached by unauthorised aircraft.

Sorry | could not be any help.

Yours sincerely
